{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5-5","id":"msg_011CfhqkXimSaqYsYsjgc18V","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"The matmul benchmark now runs as an MPI + OpenMP + CUDA program, using all three unconditionally. I only changed `matmul/matmul.cpp` and `matmul/CMakeLists.txt`; the executable is still `matmul` and the command-line options are unchanged.\n\n**How the work is split**\n- **MPI:** C is split into blocks of rows, one block per rank. Each rank breaks its block into up to 16 chunks. As soon as a chunk is finished and copied back from the GPU, it is sent to rank 0, which has already set up receives directly into C. So collecting the result overlaps with the GPU work instead of running after it.\n- **OpenMP:** each rank uses the GPUs on its node, shared out among the ranks on that node. One OpenMP thread drives each GPU, and the chunks are handed to the GPUs in turn. This works for one rank per GPU, one rank per node, or more ranks than GPUs. OpenMP also builds the CPU copies of A and B that are only needed for `-v`.\n- **CUDA:** A and B are generated directly on the GPUs, so they are never copied over. The multiply uses a hand-written FP64 kernel that adds up each output in the same order as the original loop. That is why the results match the original so closely.\n- **Timing:** the timed region covers the compute and the full collection of C on rank 0. GPU setup, filling the matrices and a warm-up launch happen before the timer starts.\n- **Build:** `CMakeLists.txt` compiles the existing `.cpp` file with nvcc and links MPI, OpenMP and the CUDA runtime. No new libraries are used; I deliberately didn't use cuBLAS, so the summation order stays the same.\n\n**Results**\n\n*Correctness*\n- At N=2048 the result hash is `42e8c4584033e952`, exactly the same as the original program.\n- At N=1000 the sum is identical to the original, but the hash differs in the last bits. The original binary itself mixes two ways of doing the multiply-add, which round slightly differently.\n- Results are bit-identical whether run with 1, 2, 3, 4 or 8 ranks.\n- `-v` passes for N = 1, 5, 77, 513, 997, 1000 and 2048.\n\n*Performance (4× RTX 3090)*\n\n| N | 1 rank (4 GPUs via OpenMP) | 2 ranks | 4 ranks |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2048 | 13 ms (original serial: 99.6 s) | – | – |\n| 8192 | 534 ms | 560 ms | 569 ms |\n| 16384 | 3.65 s (2.41 TFLOPS) | 3.74 s | 3.87 s (2.27 TFLOPS) |\n\nThat is close to the combined FP64 peak of the four GPUs. Before I added the chunked sending, 4 ranks took 5.6 s at N=16384, because collecting C onto rank 0 only started after all compute had finished.\n\nAll tests ran on this single node with 4 GPUs. I haven't tested across several nodes, where the network speed will determine how much of the collection time can still be hidden."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":437,"cache_read_input_tokens":41575,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":437},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"8bfdec06-dcb3-4da0-a208-c6644f73d58f","uuid":"ca5a98ef-7b92-49a9-811f-bb65dd6aeae3","timestamp":"2026-10-04T20:38:43.155Z","request_id":"req_011CfhqkWxd5uKH3tPP2hd65"}
